Um, I guess… things have just been harder at work lately. I do hair, and it’s like my hands don’t want to keep up with what I’m trying to do. Like they’re moving through glue or something.

My coworker even kind of laughed about it the other day, but not in a mean way, I don’t think. It just made me feel really embarrassed. That’s mostly why I came in.

I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. Like my arms don’t feel stuck exactly. It’s more like I get slowed down, if that makes sense.

I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.

Not like shaking-shaking. My hands just feel slow, I guess. Like when I’m holding the brush or the comb, it’s more that I can’t get them to move right away.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve had too much coffee my hand might feel a little weird, but that’s probably normal. My aunt used to always say caffeine made her eyelid twitch, which is kind of funny because she’d still drink like three iced coffees a day… but no, I don’t think I’m having movements I can’t control. It’s more just… slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel stiffness or resistance in your arms or legs when you try to move them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. Like my arms don’t feel stuck exactly. It’s more like I get slowed down, if that makes sense.\n\nAlthough sometimes after standing all day at the salon my legs feel kind of… tired and heavy, but that could just be because the mats at work are terrible. We had this whole thing where someone said we should get better ones, and then the manager was like, “They’re fine,” but she’s not the one standing there for hours doing foils.\n\nBut resistance when I try to move? I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your movements, like walking or getting up from a chair, feel slower than they used to, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe not so much walking or getting out of a chair. I don’t really notice it there, or maybe I’m just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s mostly my hands, especially at work. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to just happen without me thinking, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Everything takes me forever, and… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling like you're not good enough lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness, like right in my chest. Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. But that sounds normal, right?\n\nIf I haven’t done anything… I don’t know. Sometimes I still feel drained, but I don’t really keep track. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Like at work, when I’m sectioning hair or mixing color, it used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue. It’s more just… slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel stiffness or resistance in your arms or legs when you try to move them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. Like my arms don’t feel stuck exactly. It’s more like I get slowed down, if that makes sense.\n\nAlthough sometimes after standing all day at the salon my legs feel kind of… tired and heavy, but that could just be because the mats at work are terrible. We had this whole thing where someone said we should get better ones, and then the manager was like, “They’re fine,” but she’s not the one standing there for hours doing foils.\n\nBut resistance when I try to move? I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your movements, like walking or getting up from a chair, feel slower than they used to, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe not so much walking or getting out of a chair. I don’t really notice it there, or maybe I’m just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s mostly my hands, especially at work. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to just happen without me thinking, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Everything takes me forever, and… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling like you're not good enough lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness, like right in my chest. Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. But that sounds normal, right?\n\nIf I haven’t done anything… I don’t know. Sometimes I still feel drained, but I don’t really keep track. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Like at work, when I’m sectioning hair or mixing color, it used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll think I’m going at a normal pace, and then suddenly I realize it’s taking forever. People have started noticing too, which is… embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this sadness or lack of interest follows you even when you're not at work, like on your days off or when you're at home relaxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it follows me. Like, it’s not just the salon or being tired from people talking all day.\n\nEven on days off, I’ll wake up and before anything even happens, there’s already this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. Like… I’ll think about the stuff I’m supposed to want, like getting better at doing hair or having my own place, and it just feels so far away that I don’t even know why I’m trying.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at home and I’ll tell myself I’m relaxing, but it doesn’t feel relaxing. My thoughts… maybe, yeah, but I notice it more with what I’m doing than talking.

Like at work, my hands are just slow. Sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll be standing there thinking I’m doing it normally, and then I look and it’s taking forever, and then I start worrying the client can tell, and then I mess up my timing even more.

People have noticed too. Not in a mean way always, but like, “You’re still on that?” or they’ll kind of take over, and it makes me feel stupid because I know I used to be faster.

Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue.\n\nAt home… I guess I don’t pay as much attention, because nobody’s watching me there. But at work it’s obvious. Everything takes me forever now, and people are starting to notice I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking, tremors, or movements that you can’t control in your hands or other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like shaking-shaking. My hands just feel slow, I guess. Like when I’m holding the brush or the comb, it’s more that I can’t get them to move right away.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve had too much coffee my hand might feel a little weird, but that’s probably normal. My aunt used to always say caffeine made her eyelid twitch, which is kind of funny because she’d still drink like three iced coffees a day… but no, I don’t think I’m having movements I can’t control. It’s more just… slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel stiffness or resistance in your arms or legs when you try to move them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. Like my arms don’t feel stuck exactly. It’s more like I get slowed down, if that makes sense.\n\nAlthough sometimes after standing all day at the salon my legs feel kind of… tired and heavy, but that could just be because the mats at work are terrible. We had this whole thing where someone said we should get better ones, and then the manager was like, “They’re fine,” but she’s not the one standing there for hours doing foils.\n\nBut resistance when I try to move? I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your movements, like walking or getting up from a chair, feel slower than they used to, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe not so much walking or getting out of a chair. I don’t really notice it there, or maybe I’m just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s mostly my hands, especially at work. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to just happen without me thinking, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Everything takes me forever, and… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling like you're not good enough lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness, like right in my chest. Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. But that sounds normal, right?\n\nIf I haven’t done anything… I don’t know. Sometimes I still feel drained, but I don’t really keep track. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Like at work, when I’m sectioning hair or mixing color, it used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll think I’m going at a normal pace, and then suddenly I realize it’s taking forever. People have started noticing too, which is… embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this sadness or lack of interest follows you even when you're not at work, like on your days off or when you're at home relaxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it follows me. Like, it’s not just the salon or being tired from people talking all day.\n\nEven on days off, I’ll wake up and before anything even happens, there’s already this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. Like… I’ll think about the stuff I’m supposed to want, like getting better at doing hair or having my own place, and it just feels so far away that I don’t even know why I’m trying.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at home and I’ll tell myself I’m relaxing, but it doesn’t feel relaxing. It just feels like sitting there with that same “what’s the point” feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice that your speech feels slower than usual, or that your thoughts seem to drag or take longer to come, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: My thoughts… maybe, yeah, but I notice it more with what I’m doing than talking.\n\nLike at work, my hands are just slow. Sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll be standing there thinking I’m doing it normally, and then I look and it’s taking forever, and then I start worrying the client can tell, and then I mess up my timing even more.\n\nPeople have noticed too. Not in a mean way always, but like, “You’re still on that?” or they’ll kind of take over, and it makes me feel stupid because I know I used to be faster.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I used to actually like being around people more. At the salon I’d chat with clients, ask about their kids or their trips, and I used to help the newer stylists with little things, like foils or timing or whatever.

Now I mostly just keep my head down. I try not to get pulled into conversations because I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even with my friends, we used to go hiking sometimes, and I’d be the one taking pictures or making snacks, which sounds dumb, but I liked it. Now when they ask, I usually avoid it or say I’m busy.

It’s not like I hate them. It just doesn’t feel good the way it used to, and then I feel weird for not wanting to be there.

Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. But that sounds normal, right?\n\nIf I haven’t done anything… I don’t know. Sometimes I still feel drained, but I don’t really keep track. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Like at work, when I’m sectioning hair or mixing color, it used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll think I’m going at a normal pace, and then suddenly I realize it’s taking forever. People have started noticing too, which is… embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this sadness or lack of interest follows you even when you're not at work, like on your days off or when you're at home relaxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it follows me. Like, it’s not just the salon or being tired from people talking all day.\n\nEven on days off, I’ll wake up and before anything even happens, there’s already this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. Like… I’ll think about the stuff I’m supposed to want, like getting better at doing hair or having my own place, and it just feels so far away that I don’t even know why I’m trying.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at home and I’ll tell myself I’m relaxing, but it doesn’t feel relaxing. It just feels like sitting there with that same “what’s the point” feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice that your speech feels slower than usual, or that your thoughts seem to drag or take longer to come, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: My thoughts… maybe, yeah, but I notice it more with what I’m doing than talking.\n\nLike at work, my hands are just slow. Sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll be standing there thinking I’m doing it normally, and then I look and it’s taking forever, and then I start worrying the client can tell, and then I mess up my timing even more.\n\nPeople have noticed too. Not in a mean way always, but like, “You’re still on that?” or they’ll kind of take over, and it makes me feel stupid because I know I used to be faster.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t feel as rewarding or pleasurable as they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I used to actually like being around people more. At the salon I’d chat with clients, ask about their kids or their trips, and I used to help the newer stylists with little things, like foils or timing or whatever.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down. I try not to get pulled into conversations because I don’t have that… spark, I guess. Even with my friends, we used to go hiking sometimes, and I’d be the one taking pictures or making snacks, which sounds dumb, but I liked it. Now when they ask, I usually avoid it or say I’m busy.\n\nIt’s not like I hate them. It just doesn’t feel good the way it used to, and then I feel weird for not wanting to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever catch yourself moving much slower than you intend to, like your body just can't keep up with what your mind wants to do, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s mostly at work that I notice it. Like my hands just… don’t do what they’re supposed to do fast enough. Sectioning hair, mixing color, stuff I’ve done a million times, it feels like I’m moving through glue.\n\nAnd then I’ll get distracted thinking about how the client is probably noticing, or how my coworker is going to have to wait for me, and then—sorry, that’s kind of not the point—but yeah, people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be. It’s embarrassing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that happens a lot lately. I’ll be looking at the color formulas, or even just the appointment screen, and my brain kind of… goes blank.

It’s embarrassing because it’s simple stuff I used to know without thinking. Like, I’ll second-guess whether I picked the right toner or how much time I have before the next client, and then I keep checking it over and over. And then I’ll start thinking about how the front desk has that little plant now—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, focusing and deciding feels harder than it should.

But at work it’s obvious. Everything takes me forever now, and people are starting to notice I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking, tremors, or movements that you can’t control in your hands or other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like shaking-shaking. My hands just feel slow, I guess. My aunt used to always say caffeine made her eyelid twitch, which is kind of funny because she’d still drink like three iced coffees a day… but no, I don’t think I’m having movements I can’t control. It’s more just… slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel stiffness or resistance in your arms or legs when you try to move them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. We had this whole thing where someone said we should get better ones, and then the manager was like, “They’re fine,” but she’s not the one standing there for hours doing foils.\n\nBut resistance when I try to move? I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your movements, like walking or getting up from a chair, feel slower than they used to, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe not so much walking or getting out of a chair. I don’t really notice it there, or maybe I’m just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s mostly my hands, especially at work. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to just happen without me thinking, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Everything takes me forever, and… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling like you're not good enough lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness, like right in my chest. Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Now when they ask, I usually avoid it or say I’m busy.\n\nIt’s not like I hate them. It just doesn’t feel good the way it used to, and then I feel weird for not wanting to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever catch yourself moving much slower than you intend to, like your body just can't keep up with what your mind wants to do, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It’s mostly at work that I notice it. Like my hands just… don’t do what they’re supposed to do fast enough. Sectioning hair, mixing color, stuff I’ve done a million times, it feels like I’m moving through glue.\n\nAnd then I’ll get distracted thinking about how the client is probably noticing, or how my coworker is going to have to wait for me, and then—sorry, that’s kind of not the point—but yeah, people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be. It’s embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to think clearly, concentrate, or make decisions lately, even about simple things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that happens a lot lately. I’ll be looking at the color formulas, or even just the appointment screen, and my brain kind of… goes blank.\n\nIt’s embarrassing because it’s simple stuff I used to know without thinking. Like, I’ll second-guess whether I picked the right toner or how much time I have before the next client, and then I keep checking it over and over. And then I’ll start thinking about how the front desk has that little plant now—sorry, that’s not really what you asked. But yeah, focusing and deciding feels harder than it should.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt so restless or agitated that you couldn’t sit still or stay in one place for long, especially when you’re feeling down?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really restless, I guess… it’s more the opposite for me. Like at work my hands feel like they’re moving through glue. Even basic stuff, like sectioning hair or mixing color, takes me forever now.

And then I start noticing everyone around me moving normally, like the other stylists are chatting and sweeping and doing their thing, and I’m just stuck trying to finish one step. Sorry, I know you asked about sitting still—so, no, not that as much. It’s more that I’m slower than I used to be, and people have started noticing.

I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess… things have just been harder at work lately. I do hair, and it’s like my hands don’t want to keep up with what I’m trying to do. Like they’re moving through glue or something.\n\nEven simple stuff—sectioning hair, mixing color—it takes me forever now. I used to be pretty quick, and now people are starting to notice I’m slower. My coworker even kind of laughed about it the other day, but not in a mean way, I don’t think. It just made me feel really embarrassed. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed this slowness or difficulty with your hands in other situations, like when you’re getting dressed or doing something around the house?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… mostly I notice it at work because that’s where my hands have to be fast, you know? Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue.\n\nAt home… I guess I don’t pay as much attention, because nobody’s watching me there. But at work it’s obvious. Everything takes me forever now, and people are starting to notice I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking, tremors, or movements that you can’t control in your hands or other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? Not like shaking-shaking. My hands just feel slow, I guess. Like when I’m holding the brush or the comb, it’s more that I can’t get them to move right away.\n\nI mean, sometimes if I’ve had too much coffee my hand might feel a little weird, but that’s probably normal. My aunt used to always say caffeine made her eyelid twitch, which is kind of funny because she’d still drink like three iced coffees a day… but no, I don’t think I’m having movements I can’t control. It’s more just… slow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel stiffness or resistance in your arms or legs when you try to move them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really stiffness, I don’t think. Like my arms don’t feel stuck exactly. It’s more like I get slowed down, if that makes sense.\n\nAlthough sometimes after standing all day at the salon my legs feel kind of… tired and heavy, but that could just be because the mats at work are terrible. We had this whole thing where someone said we should get better ones, and then the manager was like, “They’re fine,” but she’s not the one standing there for hours doing foils.\n\nBut resistance when I try to move? I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that your movements, like walking or getting up from a chair, feel slower than they used to, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Um… maybe not so much walking or getting out of a chair. I don’t really notice it there, or maybe I’m just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s mostly my hands, especially at work. Like sectioning hair or mixing color used to just happen without me thinking, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. Everything takes me forever, and… people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, or feeling like you're not good enough lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I have. Most days I wake up and it’s just this heavy, flat sadness, like right in my chest. Like before I even check my phone or get up, I already feel like… what’s the point?\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic. My cousin used to say mornings are just awful for everybody, and she had this whole thing where she’d light a candle before work—like that was supposed to fix everything—but it’s not really like that. It’s more like the life I was trying to build, like work and getting better at hair and having my own place eventually, it just feels… pointless or too far away. Like I’m not the person who can actually do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I do get tired, but I don’t know if it’s more than what makes sense.\n\nLike, with work, standing all day and talking to people and trying to act normal, that wears me out. Even on slower days, the salon has the dryers going and everyone’s talking over each other, and by the time I get home I’m kind of done. But that sounds normal, right?\n\nIf I haven’t done anything… I don’t know. Sometimes I still feel drained, but I don’t really keep track. It’s hard to tell what counts as “energy” and what’s just me not wanting to do anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like it takes you longer to start moving, like getting up from a chair or starting to walk, even when you’re not tired or sore?\n\nPatient: Not really getting up from a chair or starting to walk, I don’t think… I mean, unless I’m just not noticing it.\n\nIt’s more my hands. Like at work, when I’m sectioning hair or mixing color, it used to be automatic, and now it’s like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll think I’m going at a normal pace, and then suddenly I realize it’s taking forever. People have started noticing too, which is… embarrassing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that this sadness or lack of interest follows you even when you're not at work, like on your days off or when you're at home relaxing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it follows me. Like, it’s not just the salon or being tired from people talking all day.\n\nEven on days off, I’ll wake up and before anything even happens, there’s already this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. Like… I’ll think about the stuff I’m supposed to want, like getting better at doing hair or having my own place, and it just feels so far away that I don’t even know why I’m trying.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at home and I’ll tell myself I’m relaxing, but it doesn’t feel relaxing. It just feels like sitting there with that same “what’s the point” feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice that your speech feels slower than usual, or that your thoughts seem to drag or take longer to come, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: My thoughts… maybe, yeah, but I notice it more with what I’m doing than talking.\n\nLike at work, my hands are just slow. Sectioning hair or mixing color used to be automatic, and now it feels like my hands are moving through glue. I’ll be standing there thinking I’m doing it normally, and then I look and it’s taking forever, and then I start worrying the client can tell, and then I mess up my timing even more.\n\nPeople have noticed too. Not in a mean way always, but like, “You’re still on that?” or they’ll kind of take over, and it makes me feel stupid because I know I used to be faster.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t feel as rewarding or pleasurable as they used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I used to actually like being around people more. It just doesn’t feel good the way it used to, and then I feel weird for not wanting to be there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever catch yourself moving much slower than you intend to, like your body just can't keep up with what your mind wants to do, even when you're not tired?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sectioning hair, mixing color, stuff I’ve done a million times, it feels like I’m moving through glue.\n\nAnd then I’ll get distracted thinking about how the client is probably noticing, or how my coworker is going to have to wait for me, and then—sorry, that’s kind of not the point—but yeah, people have started noticing I’m slower than I used to be.
